Education-oriented backers

The Texas State Teachers Association endorsed her for the November 2024 general election (TSTA 2024 list). Its PAC sent her $500 on November 18, 2024; the check was never cashed and was voided on April 16, 2025 (PAC report; void). Families for Education and Opportunity, a San Antonio political committee whose main purpose was not established, gave $1,000 on October 20, 2025, off-cycle; its other 2025 gifts went mostly to San Antonio city candidates and to Republican board member Audrey Young (PAC report; her report). For 2018, the association's profile says she was "recommended favorably by Texans for Public Education," a group of educators, and endorsed in the primary by the San Antonio Express-News (Teach the Vote). No backing from advocacy or charter groups was found for the 2024 cycle or later.

What was not found

Searched without result: her 2012 platform; statements on House Bill 2 of 2025 or teacher residencies; a recorded vote of hers on the 2024 residency rules; social-media posts; criticism of her by a named organization. Reported but not confirmed: a 2024 Texas AFT endorsement, known only from an aggregator; votes on the Permanent School Fund Corporation's board in 2023.

Gustavo Reveles, State Board of Education, District 1

Not on the 2026 ballot; term runs to January 1, 2029 (board biography).

Background and powers of the officeRecord by issueVoting recordEducation-oriented backersWhat was not found

Background and powers of the office

Reveles spent 15 years as a journalist at the El Paso Times and the Fort Worth Star-Telegram, then worked in school-district communications: Canutillo school district, 2010-2014; El Paso school district, 2014-2022; and Canutillo again from June 2022, where he is now executive director of communications (Canutillo ISD; board biography). He won the seat in November 2024 with 50.92% of the vote (San Antonio Report) and took office on January 1, 2025. He sits on the Committee on Instruction, which handles the curriculum standards (January 2025 minutes). The office's powers are set out at the start of this section (§7.101).

Record by issue

General notes

Social media

The only campaign site listed in 2024 is a Facebook group link, which was not retrieved; no X, Instagram or Bluesky account was established, and no posts were read.

Vouchers and education savings accounts

Campaign pledge

Vouchers. In his 2024 San Antonio Report questionnaire: "I strongly oppose the use of vouchers, which would further diminish funding for public schools" (San Antonio Report).

Charter schools

Campaign pledge

The state should "place a moratorium on the expansion of charter programs until the public receives a true assessment of how charter schools are performing" (San Antonio Report).

Curriculum, library books and religion in schools

Campaign pledge

Curriculum and religion. His 2024 League of Women Voters answer says "A clear separation of church and state should be a guiding principle" (2024 guide).

Statement or interview

Curriculum and the role of teachers. After his 2024 win: "We need to make sure to develop a curriculum that focuses on academics, focuses on pedagogy, and really steps away from politics and personal beliefs" (San Antonio Report). In a September 2026 interview, The Texan reported that he called teacher input on the standards an "afterthought" or a "box to check," described the process as "altered and diminished," and said: "I'm not an educator, and I would never call myself an educator" (The Texan).

Teacher preparation and certification

Campaign pledge

Asked by the League of Women Voters whether uncertified teachers should be allowed in Texas classrooms, he answered "In some cases, yes. Districts should have the option to rely on non-certified educators for hard-to-fill subjects," on condition that "an emergency certification should be followed by proper certification within a certain amount of time"; his opponent answered "No" (2024 guide). In the same cycle he wrote: "We also must make sure that voucher funds are not spent on programs led by uncertified teachers" (San Antonio Report).

Voting record

He voted against allowing every charter applicant on a named roll call in 2025 and 2026 except Unidos Soccer Leadership Academy, which he voted to allow in committee on June 25, 2025 and then voted not to veto on June 27, 2025; he also voted for vetoing Valenta Academy. He voted no on every recorded vote on the social studies standards (two of them as reported by the Tribune) and on the 2026 reading list. On the American Indian/Native Studies course he voted yes in committee, no on postponing it and yes on suspending the rules to take it up. His individual votes on certification rules are not recorded.

What was not found

How to read this document

Scope. The request was for the Democratic winners of Texas's statewide races, the State Board of Education and the Texas Supreme Court. At the requester's direction on November 9, 2026, the document adds the U.S. senator-elect and the two sitting State Board Democrats who were not on the ballot, and leaves out the winners for the Court of Criminal Appeals (criminal cases only) and the Fifteenth Court of Appeals. The Fifteenth Court has "exclusive intermediate appellate jurisdiction" over cases brought by or against state agencies, including the Texas Education Agency (TEA), the state agency that oversees public schools; the law applies to appeals perfected on or after September 1, 2024, and on that date moved to the new court the cases of that kind pending in other courts of appeals that had been filed on or after September 1, 2023 (Senate Bill 1045 of 2023).

Result status. The Associated Press has called every race here except State Board District 8 (AP results). Nothing is certified; the state's official count, the canvass, ends December 7, 2026 (Secretary of State). The record runs to November 9, 2026.

Kinds of evidence.

  • A pledge is a promise about future conduct.
  • A statement is something the person said or wrote, dated, with the venue.
  • A social media post is quoted from the platform named.
  • A recorded vote is printed in the House or Senate Journal or in State Board minutes.
  • An authored bill is one the person filed, with its outcome.
  • An official act is a vote or action in a prior office, such as a school board.
  • A court ruling is given with the judge's role, the question and the outcome.
  • A backer is an endorsement, contribution or independent expenditure, with the election stage.

Three rules matter most. A pledge is not policy. A vote on a large bill is a vote on the bill, not on each provision. A court ruling is not a policy view.

Party comparisons. Where a vote table notes that most members of the person's party in that chamber, or on the State Board, voted the other way, the note comes from this review's own count of the journal or minutes roll call. It is given so the reader can see where a person's vote differed from most of their party's; exact counts are not given.

How sources appear. Links sit in the text. A quotation or record with no qualifier was confirmed on the linked page; "according to" or "as reported by" marks something taken from a news account or other secondary source.

What "nothing found" means. The named sources were searched without result; it does not mean the person has no view.

Limits. Posts on X, Facebook and Instagram could not be read for most people here; each profile lists the accounts checked. Some news pages, aggregator sites and Ballotpedia questionnaires could not be opened. State Board minutes record individual votes for only some decisions; most show only "carried" or a tally (minutes index).

Not confirmed, and sources that could not be opened

These items were reported but could not be confirmed on a page this review could read. They are listed so a reader can follow them up; none is used as fact in the profiles.

  • Hinojosa: a report that she refused to sign the August 2023 report of the House select committee on vouchers.
  • Hinojosa: a quotation on uncertified teachers credited to her in Texas Observer coverage of an April 3, 2025 House Public Education hearing.
  • Hinojosa: a report of criticism by the American Federation for Children's chief executive after the election.
  • Goodwin: a post-election television report of her statements on vouchers and on state takeovers of districts.
  • Talarico: an April 2026 Texas Observer essay on campaign money from Leadership for Educational Equity and on Teach For America's summer training institute.
  • Talarico: a reported August 2024 hearing remark on training teachers in classroom management.
  • Talarico: contributions from John and Laura Arnold.
  • Johnson: a report that he tried to remove vouchers from House Bill 100 in 2023.
  • Johnson: an October 2026 Atlantic interview on defending laws.
  • Johnson, Eckhardt, Ellis and Kelly: Houston Federation of Teachers endorsements listed only by third-party or aggregator sites.
  • Eckhardt: a report that she would "immediately curtail" the comptroller's spending reviews of Prosper ISD and other districts.
  • Flores: a report that he called vouchers "unconstitutional".
  • Van de Walker: a 2026 primary-guide answer calling for "routes to alternate certification that actually work" and an end to vouchers.
  • Pérez-Díaz and Reveles: 2024 Texas AFT endorsements known only from aggregator sites.
  • Reveles: a KVIA quotation on certification.

Sources that could not be opened or read:

  • Posts on X: Hinojosa's accounts returned an access error; Goodwin's and Talarico's posts were read only where news articles or campaign pages embedded them; Johnson's and Eckhardt's could not be retrieved.
  • Facebook, Instagram, Threads and TikTok accounts, which require a login, for most people in this document, including all four judges and most State Board members.
  • The New York Times profile of Talarico cited by the Daily Wire; two Texas Observer pages (on Hinojosa and Talarico); a KERA page on Eckhardt; a Texas Monthly interview with Hinojosa.
  • Tucker's two Ballotpedia questionnaires and Van de Walker's 2026 primary guide.
  • A Texas Association of School Administrators table on a 2020 charter vote by Pérez-Díaz, which reportedly shows a different position from the one in the board minutes and was not read for this review.
  • Aggregator sites listing judicial and State Board endorsements.
